When such a ruler dies, the people mourn, children stop singing, and even laborers stop humming. That is virtue.”
-   S78: “Is my lack of virtue such a serious flaw?”
-   S79: “It is serious. At this rate, you will not live long. Harsh laws may let you establish authority by crushing petty villains, but over time resentment and anger will accumulate.”
-   S80: “Should I fear the resentment of ordinary people?”

-   S83: “The same problem will remain when your superior arrives or when a successor replaces you. You changed the law, established your authority, and accumulated achievements at frightening speed. Local factions are grinding their teeth over it. Other cities have their own underworld powers as well. Considering how many interests you have offended, I doubt you will live long if you continue like this.”
-   S84: “Then what do you think I should do?”
-   S85: “You are as precarious as morning dew. If you want to live, resign as Deputy Magistrate, return your authority to House Baskerville, and let the Patriarch and Imperial Family take credit for what happened here. Then go somewhere appropriate for your age—training in Le Rouge et Le Noir Mountain, perhaps, or the Imperial Academy—and prepare for the future.”
